Let me carefully work through this audit. I need to go row by row for all 52 cases (Part 1 is all of them since the list includes O1-O52). For each row I need to:

1. Write BLIND and BLIND MARK from my blind reading
2. Copy the tester's MARK line
3. ON VERDICT: AGREE or DISAGREE with the other reader's VERDICT
4. ON QUOTES: SUPPORTS, PARTLY or FAILS
5. ON SEARCH: for SILENT rows, run their recorded search; for others NONE
6. ON MARK: AGREE or DISAGREE with the other reader's MARK
7. YOUR MARK: my mark after reading both
8. QUOTE lines (up to 3)
9. WHY

First, let me establish my blind marks for each case. My blind readings give findings; I need to derive the mark against the fixed verdict using the marking rules:

1. CASE DISPUTED: the situation itself, read in ordinary terms before any theory, decides a point against the fixed verdict.
2. DISAGREE: on at least one point, the theory's verdict reaches a different finding from the fixed verdict.
3. SPLIT: on at least one point, the theory's sentences support two readings that reach different findings, and the text leaves the choice open.
4. SILENT: on at least one point, the theory leaves the finding open, because it takes the deciding matter as an input the case leaves unstated, or because its sentences stop short of deciding it.
5. AGREE: on every point, the theory's verdict reaches the same finding as the fixed verdict.

Now let me derive blind marks:

The key question: what makes something SPLIT vs SILENT?

SPLIT: "the theory's sentences support two readings that reach different findings, and the text leaves the choice between them open." - This is about the theory's text being interpretable in two ways.

SILENT: "the theory leaves the finding open, because it takes the deciding matter as an input the case leaves unstated, or because its sentences stop short of deciding it." - This is about missing inputs or indeterminate text.

For O10: the deciding matter is the contract / modeling. The contract is literally a "declared index" - an input. The case leaves it unstated. So SILENT fits precisely and directly. Does SPLIT fit? "The theory's sentences support two readings" - the theory's sentences here are consistent and clear (kinds = signatures on contracts). They don't support two readings of themselves. What varies is the input (contract). So SPLIT does NOT fit! The variation comes from input values, not text interpretation.

Whereas in my blind O1: "Whether each restated rule is an account or a table of successes: if the rule encodes the response to every admitted change it is an account... if it tabulates observed answers it fails (F1)..." - this is a question of which factual description applies (does the rule encode responses or tabulate answers?) - also input-ish. Hmm!!! Wait, let me parse the sentence super carefully: "What makes a restriction appropriate to the question asked is a substantive, criticizable part of the claim; the semantics records the restriction and supplies no rule that certifies it."

Reading A: "What makes a restriction appropriate [is X]" = the criterion for appropriateness is X = "a substantive, criticizable part of the claim" = the restriction must be backed by a substantive, criticizable part of the claim. Then "; the semantics records the restriction and supplies no rule that certifies it" = the semantics doesn't certify (no automatic guarantee) - the appropriateness is judged by whether the ground is substantive and criticizable.

Reading B: "What makes a restriction appropriate to the question asked is a substantive, criticizable part of the claim" = the question of what makes it appropriate is itself a substantive criticizable part of the claim (i.e., the appropriateness-question is part of the claim and open to criticism), "; the semantics records the restriction and supplies no rule that certifies it" = no certification supplied.

Hmm!!! Reading B is the tester's ("the semantics records restrictions and supplies no rule that certifies them"). Reading A gives a criterion!! Hmm!! Which reading? "
